igamt-review 21

Reviewed comparison of the narrative with the IGAMT conformance export.

Show 21 findings
  • Issue 3.3 Acknowledgement (ACK) Message Profile: IZ-41 and IZ-42 on the shared MSH flavor apply to every message, and no message can pass both. All eight profiles use one MSH flavor, MSH_#ConsolidatedIG_IZ01. It carries IZ-41 (MSH-16 SHALL be 'AL') and IZ-42 (MSH-15 SHALL be 'NE') as segment-level constraints, so both apply to VXU, QBP, ACK, and RSP alike. The drafted examples follow the usual pattern (VXU and QBP with MSH-15 'ER' and MSH-16 'AL'; ACK and RSP with 'NE'/'NE'), and every one fails one of the two. Z22 also has its own message-level IZ-41 with slightly different wording.
    IGAMT export defectAction: IGAMT
    IGAMT export
    • MSH_#ConsolidatedIG_IZ01: IZ-41 "MSH-16 (Application Acknowledgment Type) shall contain the value 'AL'"; IZ-42 "MSH-15 (Accept Acknowledgment Type) shall contain the value 'NE'".
    • Z22 (message level): IZ-41 "MSH-16 shall contain the value 'AL'".
    Recommendation Move the acknowledgment-type constraints to the message profiles that need them (for example, VXU and QBP: MSH-16 'AL'; ACK and RSP: MSH-15 and MSH-16 'NE'), and say in the narrative which values each message uses. The example checks skip IZ-41 and IZ-42 until this is fixed (quality/config.yml).
    qf-igamt-msh-ack-constraints
  • Issue 3.5.2.5 Z42 - Return Evaluated Immunization History and Forecast: Z42's co-constraint table has no conditions, so every history dose needs a forecast group. Z22 and Z32 select a co-constraint table by the kind of Order Segment Group (administered, refused, …). Z42 has one unconditional table. Its "Immunization Forecast Results" group is R [1..*], so it applies to every ORC/RXA group in the response: each returned dose would need a forecast, and evaluation results are allowed in the forecast group. Z42 also has no rows for eligibility, funding, VIS, comments, or contraindications, which Z32 returns.
    IGAMT export defectAction: IGAMT
    Narrative 4.7.8.1: "All forecast information SHALL be returned under a single ORC/RXA order group." 4.4.4.5 and 4.4.4.6 separate "Immunization Event with Evaluation" from "Forecast".
    IGAMT export
    • Z42 OBX at HISTORY_FORECAST.HISTORY_FORECAST_OBSERVATION: one simple table; groups Immunization Evaluation Results S [0..*], Immunization Forecast Results R [1..*], Conditions: Patient Observations O [0..*].
    Recommendation Make the Z42 tables conditional like Z32: evaluation for administered or historical doses (RXA-20 CP or PA), and forecast for the CVX 998 group. Add the event-level observations that Z32 allows.
    qf-igamt-z42-unconditional
  • Issue 4.4.2.3 OBX-2 Value Type: Co-constraints fix OBX-2 to one exact value, which contradicts "accept both CE and CWE" and "responses SHOULD use CE". Every co-constraint row tests OBX-2 for an exact value (for example 'CE'). A submitter that sends CWE for a coded observation fails validation, although the narrative says systems SHALL accept both. Patient Condition (75323-6) is fixed to 'CWE' in Z22, Z32, and Z42, while every other coded observation is 'CE'. An IIS that follows the narrative's SHOULD (use CE in responses) fails Z32/Z42 validation for that observation. Free-text Comment (48767-8) is 'CE' in Z22 but 'ST' in Z32.
    ConflictAction: Narrative and IGAMTAnswers Word comment 27
    Narrative "IIS responding to QBP SHOULD use CE when returning coded observations … Systems … SHALL accept both CE and CWE where a coded value is expected and SHALL process them equivalently." Comment 27: "Double-check that right data type is used in co-constraint tables"; comment 28: "Does this need to be reflected in the co-constraint tables?"
    IGAMT export
    • Co-constraint OBX-2 checks are PlainText equality (one value).
    • 75323-6: OBX-2 = CWE in Z22 (three tables), Z32, Z42.
    • 48767-8: OBX-2 = CE in Z22 tables 1, 3, 4; ST in Z32 tables 2, 3, 4.
    Recommendation Yes, it needs to be reflected (comment 28). Allow both CE and CWE where the narrative says both are accepted (a value-set check on OBX-2 instead of exact text), or narrow the narrative. Set 48767-8 to a text type (ST or TX) in every profile. Decide whether 75323-6 should be CE like the rest.
    qf-igamt-c27-c28-ce-cwe
  • Issue 4.5.2.5 Data Type and Structure: Newborn ('NB') names are not held to the family-and-given rule, and other names are over-constrained when an 'L' name is present. The profiles implement the two XPN flavors by slicing the whole PID segment on PID-5.7, not by name repetition. If any repetition is 'L', every repetition (nickname, maiden, and so on) must use XPN_IZ01 and have both family and given name. If no repetition is 'L', every repetition uses XPN_IZ02, where only one of family or given is needed, so an 'NB' placeholder name with one part passes. The two slice conditions also overlap: a patient with an 'L' and an 'N' name matches both. Slicing exists only in Z22; the response profiles (Z31, Z32) always use PID_IZ01 and Z42 uses PID_IZ02.
    ConflictAction: IGAMTAnswers Word comment 36
    Narrative "Official registry and newborn names must contain both a family and given name, while other types (e.g., nickname, maiden) may have only one component." Word comment 36 asks to confirm three statements: (1) XPN.1 and XPN.2 populated for 'L' and 'NB' names; (2) XPN.7 populated for all repetitions; (3) cardinalities and flavors as defined in the data type section.
    IGAMT export
    • Slicing (Z22, PID): PID_IZ01 if at least one PID-5.7 = 'L'; PID_IZ03 if at least one PID-5.7 is not 'L'.
    • PID_IZ01.PID-5 uses XPN_IZ01: XPN.1 R, XPN.2 R, XPN.7 R.
    • PID_IZ03.PID-5 uses XPN_IZ02: XPN.1 C(R/RE) if XPN.2 not valued, XPN.2 RE, XPN.7 R.
    • Z31/Z32 PID_IZ01 (XPN_IZ01) and Z42 PID_IZ02 are not sliced.
    Recommendation Statement (2) exists: XPN.7 is R in every PID-5 flavor. Statement (1) holds for 'L' only. Ask IGAMT whether a slice or co-constraint can apply per repetition (by XPN.7). If not, use a conformance statement such as "each PID-5 repetition with XPN.7 in ('L','NB') SHALL value XPN.1 and XPN.2". Make the two slice conditions mutually exclusive. Decide whether responses should follow the same rule. Statement (3) has no counterpart in the narrative yet; the card should state it once the data type pages exist.
    qf-igamt-c36-name-flavors
  • Issue 4.6.1.5 Use of Administered vs. Historical on Output: Text says RSP should leave RXA-9 empty; Z32 and Z42 require RXA-9 for completed doses. The narrative prefers an empty RXA-9 in responses. In Z32 and Z42, RXA-9 is conditional and becomes Required whenever RXA-20 is CP or PA, which is every administered or historical dose returned. An IIS that follows the preferred approach fails validation.
    ConflictAction: CDC decision
    Narrative "Leaving RXA-9 empty on outbound RSP messages SHOULD be the preferred approach."
    IGAMT export
    • Z32 RXA_IZ02 and Z42 RXA_IZ03, RXA-9: C(R/O), predicate: RXA-20 in ('CP','PA').
    Recommendation CDC decides. Either change the response flavors to make RXA-9 O (or RE) in Z32/Z42, or replace the narrative's preference with what to send (for example, always '00' or '01').
    qf-igamt-rxa9-rsp
  • Issue 4.8.6.2 HL7 v2 Representation: Patient conditions: the text uses 30945-0 in the no-vaccination group; the profiles only allow 75323-6 there. Section 4.8.6.2 says conditions are sent as 30945-0 (contraindication/precaution) observations in an ORC/RXA group that reports no vaccination. Section 4.4.4.4 and Figure 20 say patient conditions use 75323-6 in the Patient Observations group. The co-constraint tables follow 4.4.4.4: in the no-vaccination (CVX 998) group, 30945-0 is not allowed, and 75323-6 (with 85585-8 and 88878-4) is. 30945-0 is only allowed in the contraindicated group (NA with a real CVX).
    ConflictAction: Narrative and IGAMT
    Narrative 4.8.6.2: "conditions are expressed using existing vaccination contraindication/precaution LOINC codes … 30945-0 … REQUIRED to convey the condition itself"; 4.8.6.3: "Submitting systems SHALL include LOINC 30945-0 with a valid SNOMED CT value to convey a condition."
    IGAMT export
    • Z22 no-vaccination table (RXA-20 NA, CVX 998, RXA-6 999): 31044-1, 75505-8, 59784-9, group 75323-6/85585-8/88878-4.
    • Z22 contraindicated table (RXA-20 NA, CVX ≠ 998): group 30945-0/30946-8/30944-3/48767-8.
    Recommendation CDC decides which representation is current, then aligns sections 4.4.4.4 and 4.8.6, Figure 20, and the co-constraint tables.
    qf-igamt-condition-loinc
  • Warning 2.7.5 Administering Organization (RXA-11): "SHALL populate RXA-11" for administered doses, but the administered flavor has RXA-11 as RE. The narrative makes RXA-11 mandatory for administered vaccinations. In the administered flavor (RXA_IZ01_Adm) RXA-11 is RE, so a message without it passes validation. RXA-11.4 (Facility) is R within LA2_IZ01, which only applies when RXA-11 is sent.
    ConflictAction: CDC decision
    Narrative "For administered vaccinations, systems SHALL populate RXA-11 with the administering organization."
    IGAMT export
    • RXA_IZ01_Adm RXA-11: RE [0..1] LA2_IZ01; RXA_IZ01: C(RE/O) when RXA-20 in ('CP','PA') and RXA-9.1 = '00'.
    • RXA_IZ01_His RXA-11: O (consistent with "RXA-11 SHOULD be empty" for historical doses).
    Recommendation Make RXA-11 R in the administered flavor (and the RE side of the RXA_IZ01 predicate R), or soften the statement to SHOULD. With usage "R" now defined as "IIS reports an ERR if missing", R matches the intent.
    qf-igamt-rxa11-administered
  • Warning 2.8.2 Date of Birth (PID-7): The text allows a time-zone offset in PID-7; the profiles reject one. PID-7 uses TS_NZ, whose time pattern (DTM_NZ) has no offset, so a birth date/time with an offset fails format validation. The narrative tells senders to use the birth time zone and tells IIS to ignore any offset present. RXA-3 and OBX-14 also use TS_NZ. Their wording ("use the local time zone") is consistent if it means local time without an offset.
    ConflictAction: Narrative and IGAMT
    Narrative "if it is included, PID-7 SHALL be encoded using the time zone where the birth occurred." "When time or time-zone components are present in PID-7, receiving IIS SHALL ignore the time and offset components …"
    IGAMT export
    • PID_IZ01/PID_IZ03 PID-7: R [1..1] TS_NZ; DTM_NZ pattern YYYYMMDD[HH[MM[SS[.S[S[S[S]]]]]]] (no offset).
    • Z42 PID_IZ02 PID-7 uses TS_IZ01 instead.
    Recommendation Say "local time of birth, without a time-zone offset" in the narrative. If offsets must be tolerated, loosen the flavor. Align Z42 with the other profiles.
    qf-igamt-pid7-time-zone
  • Warning 2.9.1 Usage Designations and Expectations: Co-constraint tables use usage 'S', which the guide does not define. Eighteen co-constraint rows and groups have usage 'S' (for example, VIS groups, the contraindication group, and the forecast dates). The guide defines R, RE, O, C, X, and IX. A reader cannot tell whether 'S' means "should", "situational", or something else, and Figure 20 shows these items as Required.
    GapAction: Narrative and IGAMT
    IGAMT export
    • Usage 'S' in Z22 (VIS groups, historical eligibility, contraindication group and rows), Z32 (eligibility, VIS, evaluation, forecast dates), Z42 (evaluation group, forecast dates).
    Recommendation Define 'S' in this section (as IGAMT uses it), or replace it in the tables with a defined usage.
    qf-igamt-usage-s
  • Warning 3.5.2.2 Z32 - Return Complete Immunization History: Z32 (history only) contains evaluation and forecast groups.     Recommendation Remove the evaluation and forecast groups from Z32 (or state that Z32 may carry them) and correct the cross-reference in 4.7.7.5 to Z42.
    qf-igamt-z32-evaluation
  • Warning 4.4.4 Order Segment Group Flavors: The Order Segment Group flavors (text and Figure 20) and the co-constraint tables do not match. The flavor sections and Figure 20 list which observations belong in each Order Segment Group. The co-constraint tables allow more in some places and less in others. Main differences: serological immunity, history of disease, and patient condition are allowed in the administered and historical event groups (the text puts them only in Patient Observations). In Z32 they are allowed in every group. The contraindication set is required in Figure 20 but optional in the tables. Indication observations are missing from the historical-event table. Z32, the history-only response, has evaluation and forecast groups. Z42 has a single table with no conditions (see the Z42 finding). The cell-by-cell comparison is on the comparison page.
    ConflictAction: Narrative and IGAMTAnswers Word comment 31
    Narrative Sections 4.4.4.1–4.4.4.6 and Figure 20. Comments 30 and 31: "Confirm that the co-constraint tables address this section"; "Make sure these are in alignment with the Co-constraint tables".
    IGAMT export
    • Z22 administered table: also allows 75505-8, 59784-9, 75323-6 group; 31044-1 appears twice.
    • Z22 contraindicated table: contraindication group usage S [0..*].
    • Z32 table 5 (no condition) allows 31044-1, 75505-8, 59784-9, 75323-6 in every group.
    • Z32 tables 1 and 2 contain Immunization Forecast Results and Immunization Evaluation Results groups.
    Recommendation Agree with CDC which is intended, then align both. The text and Figure 20 are the more restrictive and readable statement, so the tables probably need to change. Once aligned, generate the figure from the co-constraint tables so they cannot drift apart again.
    qf-igamt-c30-c31-figure-20
  • Warning 4.5.2.11 Non-Patient Person Names: ORC-12 (Ordering Provider) has usage C with no predicate. In ORC_IZ01 (Z22, Z32), ORC-12 is conditional, but there is no predicate, so neither readers nor validators know when it is required. Z42's ORC_IZ02 has it as O.

  • Warning 4.5.5.1 Value Sets and Field Usage: "Use the CDCREC value set": the profiles bind category-only value sets, without MENA or the detailed codes. The narrative talks about "the CDCREC value set", including detailed subcategories and Middle Eastern or North African (MENA) as a top-level race category. The profiles bind PID-10 to PHVS_RaceCategory_CDC_NullFlavor (6 OMB categories, Other Race, and three unknown codes) and PID-22 to PHVS_EthnicityGroup_CDC_Unk. Neither contains MENA or any detailed code. Both are Static/Open, so a validator would likely only warn, but readers cannot see what is allowed.
    GapAction: Narrative and IGAMT
    Narrative "Race (PID-10) SHALL use the CDCREC value set." "IIS SHOULD support the MENA race code as a top-level category." "IIS SHALL accept additional valid CDCREC codes."
    IGAMT export
    • PID-10: PHVS_RaceCategory_CDC_NullFlavor (9 codes: 1002-5, 2028-9, 2054-5, 2076-8, 2106-3, 2131-1, ASKU, PHC1175, UNK); Static, Open.
    • PID-22: PHVS_EthnicityGroup_CDC_Unk (5 codes); Static, Open.
    Recommendation Name the bound value sets in the narrative (or rename them in IGAMT). Decide whether MENA and the detailed CDCREC codes belong in the binding (Dynamic), and whether 2131-1 Other Race remains.
    qf-igamt-race-cdcrec
  • Warning 4.6.6.3 Reporting VIS Information in HL7 v2 Messages: Z22 administered doses cannot carry the VIS publication date (29768-9) for the alternate VIS method. The alternate VIS method is a CVX (30956-7), the publication date (29768-9), and the date given (29769-7). In the Z22 administered-dose table, the "VIS Information: Vaccine Type and Dates" group lists 29769-7 twice and 29768-9 not at all. The historical-dose table has it right. The Z22 administered document-type group also has no OBX-5 binding for 69764-9; the historical group binds PHVS_VISBarcodes_IIS. Constraint IZ-36 covers the barcode check separately.
    IGAMT export defectAction: IGAMT
    Narrative "Under the alternate method, submitters report the CVX Unspecified Formulation code representing the applicable VIS, along with an OBX giving the VIS publication date and an OBX giving the VIS date-given."
    IGAMT export
    • Z22 table 1, group VIS Information: Vaccine Type and Dates: 30956-7, 29769-7, 29769-7.
    • Z22 table 2 (historical), same group: 30956-7, 29768-9, 29769-7.
    Recommendation Replace the second 29769-7 row with 29768-9, and bind 69764-9 to PHVS_VISBarcodes_IIS in the administered group.
    qf-igamt-vis-dates
  • Warning 4.7.7.4 Dose Validity and Reason for Validity: "Y or N are the only allowable values" for dose validity is not enforced. The dose-validity observation (59781-5) is typed ID with no value set in the Z32 and Z42 co-constraints, so any value passes.
    GapAction: IGAMT
    Narrative "'Y' or 'N' SHALL be used in OBX-5 as the only allowable values when dose validity is reported"
    IGAMT export
    • Z42 and Z32 Immunization Evaluation Results: 59781-5 OBX-2 = ID; no OBX-5 binding.
    • HL70136_IZ01 (Y, N) exists and is used for PD1-12.
    Recommendation Bind OBX-5 for 59781-5 to HL70136_IZ01 (or an equivalent Y/N value set).
    qf-igamt-dose-validity
  • Warning 4.7.8.2 Vaccine Type and Forecast Initiation: Forecast observations: the text and the Z42 table list different LOINC codes. The narrative allows the legacy 30979-9 (Vaccines Due Next) after 30956-7, but the Z42 forecast group has no row for it, so it cannot be sent. The Z42 forecast group allows 93123-8 and 93122-0, which the Forecast section (4.4.4.6) does not mention. The status observation 59783-1 is bound to Imm_status in Z32 but has no binding in Z42.
    GapAction: Narrative and IGAMT
    Narrative "If needed to support legacy systems, IIS SHOULD include the legacy LOINC 30979-9 (Vaccines Due Next) after LOINC 30956-7 (Vaccine Type)." 4.4.4.6 lists 30956-7, 59783-1, 30982-3, 30981-5, 30980-7, 59778-1, 59777-3, 59779-9, 59780-7, 59782-3, 30973-2.
    IGAMT export
    • Z42 Immunization Forecast Results: 30956-7, 59783-1, 30982-3, 30981-5, 30980-7, 59778-1, 59777-3, 59779-9, 59780-7, 59782-3, 30973-2, 93123-8, 93122-0.
    Recommendation Add 30979-9 to the forecast group (optional), describe 93123-8 and 93122-0 in 4.4.4.6, and bind 59783-1 in Z42.
    qf-igamt-forecast-loinc
  • Warning 4.8.6.2 HL7 v2 Representation: "A SNOMED CT code from PHVS_VaccinationContraindication_IIS", but 12 of the value set's 23 codes are not SNOMED CT. The value set mixes 11 SNOMED CT codes with 12 CDC PHIN VS (CDCPHINVS) codes. A sender can use a CDCPHINVS code and pass validation while breaking the narrative's SHALL; or follow the narrative and be unable to express the concepts that only have CDCPHINVS codes.
    ConflictAction: Narrative and IGAMT
    Narrative "OBX-5 SHALL contain a SNOMED CT code from the PHVS_VaccinationContraindication_IIS value set"
    IGAMT export
    • PHVS_VaccinationContraindication_IIS: 23 codes; systems SCT (11), CDCPHINVS (12).
    Recommendation Say "a code from the PHVS_VaccinationContraindication_IIS value set", or update the value set to SNOMED CT only.
    qf-igamt-contraindication-snomed
  • Warning 4.8.7.2 Required Data Elements: The refusal slice also requires RXA-6 = 999, which the refusal section does not list. The Z22 slicing selects the refusal flavor (RXA_IZ01_Ref) only when RXA-20 = 'RE' and RXA-6 = '999', and constraint IZ-48 requires RXA-6 = 999 whenever RXA-20 = 'RE'. The refusal section's list of minimum elements leaves out RXA-6. A refusal sent with any other amount is not validated against the refusal flavor (so RXA-18 is not checked as required) and fails IZ-48. The same applies to contraindicated (NA, CVX ≠ 998) and no-vaccination (NA, CVX 998) groups, which both also select on RXA-6 = 999.
    GapAction: NarrativeAnswers Word comment 57
    Narrative "The minimum required elements are: ORC-3=9999 – Filler Order Number; RXA-3 …; RXA-5 …; RXA-18 – Reason for refusal; RXA-20 = 'RE'." Comment 57: "Double-check this is how the slicing logic is set up."
    IGAMT export
    • Slice RXA_IZ01_Ref: RXA-20 = 'RE' AND RXA-6 = '999'; RXA-18 R [1..*] in this flavor.
    • IZ-48 (RXA_IZ01): if RXA-20 = 'RE' then RXA-6 SHALL be '999'.
    • IZ-45 (Z22 message): if RXA-20 in ('NA','RE') then ORC-3.1 SHALL be '9999'.
    Recommendation The slicing is set up consistently with IZ-45 and IZ-48. Add "RXA-6 = 999 – Administered amount" to the refusal list, and state the RXA-6/ORC-3 values in the non-administration sections too. Consider whether the slice should key on RXA-20 alone, so that a wrong RXA-6 is reported by IZ-48 rather than silently changing which flavor applies.
    qf-igamt-c57-refusal-slice
  • Note 4.5.4.1 General Principles: "SHALL send" patient address, phone, and guardian contact, which the profiles have as RE. PID-11, PID-13, and NK1-5 are RE (send when known). The statements read as unconditional. Adding "when known" matches the profiles and the guide's definition of RE.
    QuestionAction: Narrative
    Narrative "Systems SHALL send patient contact information in PID-13 and guardian or next-of-kin contact information in NK1-5." "Systems SHALL send patient address information in PID-11."
    IGAMT export
    • PID-11 RE [0..*] XAD_IZ02; PID-13 RE [0..*] XTN_IZ01; NK1-5 RE [0..*] XTN_IZ01 (Z22).
    Recommendation Add "when known" to these statements.
    qf-igamt-contact-address-re
  • Note 4.6.5 Funding Source and Eligibility: References to Chapter 7 point to content that now comes from IGAMT, and use different section numbers. Cards point to "HL7 field references", "Chapter 7 (OBX table)", "Section 7.1.4, Z32", "Section 7.1.7, co-constraint table", "Section 7.2.18", and "Section 7.4, Value Sets". Chapter 7 is not in the narrative draft; on the website it is generated from the IGAMT export, so these become links to specific profile, co-constraint, and value-set pages. The section numbers are also inconsistent with each other (7.1.4, 7.1.7, and 7.2.18 are all given as the co-constraint tables).
    Narrative defectAction: NarrativeAnswers Word comment 49
    Narrative "(see HL7 field references)"; "See Chapter 7, Message Infrastructure, and the OBX co-constraint tables, Section 7.2.18"; "co-constraints table in Chapter 7.1.4, Z32"; "Section 7.1.7, co-constraint table". Comment 49: "Is this referring to Section 7?"
    Recommendation Yes. Replace section numbers with managed links (by ID) to the generated pages: the Z22 OBX co-constraint table for funding, the Z42 table for evaluation and forecast, and value-set pages for race and ethnicity.
    qf-igamt-c49-chapter-7
  • Note 4.6.6.3 Reporting VIS Information in HL7 v2 Messages: VIS groups appear for historical doses; the narrative describes VIS only for administered doses. The Z22 historical-dose table repeats both VIS groups (usage S [1..*]) and the eligibility/funding group (S [1..1]). The narrative and Figure 20 describe VIS and eligibility for new administrations. A reader cannot tell whether VIS information on a historical dose is expected, allowed, or meaningless.
    QuestionAction: CDC decision
    Narrative "VIS information is reported using one or more OBX segments within the administered vaccination group (ORC/RXA/RXR)."
    IGAMT export
    • Z22 table 2 (RXA-9 01–08): groups Dose-Level Program Eligibility and Funding Status S [1..1], VIS Information: Document Type S [1..*], VIS Information: Vaccine Type and Dates S [1..*].
    Recommendation Decide; then either remove these groups from the historical table or say in the narrative that they are allowed.
    qf-igamt-vis-historical

ta-review 19

A question asked in technical assistance that the current guide does not fully answer (working analysis, local builds only).

Show 19 findings
  • Warning 2.1.3 Message Structure and Terminology: TA question (MSH Message Header, MSH-10 Message Control ID): Gap. MSH-10 is required, but the guide never says it must be unique (the past answer says per sender, at least per day) or that MSA-2 echoes it. The query tag in QPD-2 is not discussed either.
    Gap
    Asked How commonly do EHRs populate the query tag (QPD-2) and message control ID (MSH-10) with unique IDs versus static values, and how should a receiving IIS treat them?
    Recommendation In a Message Header section, say MSH-10 SHALL be unique for the sender and is echoed in MSA-2.

    See the TA question

    qf-ta-q20-004
  • Warning 2.1.3 Message Structure and Terminology: TA question (MSH Message Header, MSH-6 Receiving Facility): Gap. The guide says nothing about MSH-3, MSH-5 or MSH-6 values: that they are agreed between trading partners, that an IIS should require specific values only when it needs them for routing, or which party to name when an HIE passes a message through. TA has answered this the same way several times.
    Gap
    Asked When an HIE is a pass-through for immunization queries, should senders put the HIE or the IIS (the ultimate destination) in MSH-6?
    Recommendation Add a short Message Header section to chapter 2 covering MSH-3, MSH-5, MSH-6 and MSH-23: agreed during onboarding, required by an IIS only when needed for routing or processing, not changed by intermediaries, and in an ACK or RSP normally echoing the sender's MSH-3 and MSH-4.

    See the TA question

    qf-ta-q20-015
  • Warning 2.7.3 Responsible Organization (MSH-22): TA question (MSH Message Header, MSH-22 Sending Responsible Organization): Partly answers. The guide ties transport credentials to MSH-4 and says authorization to submit for another organization is set up at onboarding. It does not say whether an IIS should check MSH-22 against that authorization, or what severity to give a mismatch.
    Partly answers
    Asked Is there national guidance on how an IIS should respond when the MSH-22 value in a VXU has no relationship to the submitter's SOAP credentials or the MSH-4 sending facility? Should this be an error or warning, and how should MSH-4, MSH-22, ORC-17 and RXA-11 relate?
    Recommendation Add a statement on MSH-22 that is not authorized for the submitting organization: for example, IIS MAY validate it against onboarding records, and report a mismatch as an Error or Warning by local policy.

    See the TA question

    qf-ta-q38-003
  • Warning 3.3.2 Application-Level Scope of ACK Messages: TA question (MSH Message Header, MSH-15 / MSH-16 Accept and Application Acknowledgment Type): Partly answers. IGAMT fixes MSH-15 to 'NE' and MSH-16 to 'AL', but the narrative never explains acknowledgement modes. 3.3.2 has an intermediary return 'CR' (a commit acknowledgement), which the sender's MSH-15 'NE' says it does not want.
    Partly answers
    Asked The Release 1.5 IG says enhanced acknowledgement mode is not supported. An IIS returns original-mode codes (AA, AE, AR) in MSA-1 regardless of MSH-15 (previously asking senders for AL), rather than enhanced-mode codes (CA, CE, CR). Is this correct, and will enhanced mode remain unsupported?
    Recommendation Explain in the ACK profile that MSH-15 'NE' and MSH-16 'AL' mean one application acknowledgement and no commit acknowledgement, and square that with the 'CR' an intermediary may return.

    See the TA question

    qf-ta-q01-007
  • Warning 3.3.4.1 MSA-1 Guidance: TA question (MSH Message Header, MSH-9 Message Type): Partly answers. The MSA-1 guidance says 'AR' was "previously" used for an unsupported message type, event code or processing ID, and removes only the fourth reason. It never says, in the present tense, that these conditions still get 'AR', or that "message type" covers all of MSH-9 (code, trigger event, structure). AIRA promised this clarification in 2024.
    Partly answers
    Asked The IG says to return AR for an unsupported message type. Does "message type" refer to the whole MSG data type in MSH-9, so that an unsupported trigger event (MSH-9.2) and message structure (MSH-9.3) are also covered?
    Recommendation Add a statement: an IIS SHALL return 'AR' when any component of MSH-9, MSH-11 or MSH-12 is not supported, with an ERR segment naming the field.

    See the TA question

    qf-ta-q33-020
  • Warning 3.5.2.1 Z23 - Return an Acknowledgement: TA question (MSH Message Header, MSH-21 Message Profile Identifier): Differs. Asked in 2019 and 2020 and answered: return a Z33 RSP, not a Z23. The guide now says a query that cannot be processed (malformed MSH or QPD, missing required header information) gets a Z23 ACK, and IGAMT allows MSH-9.2 'Q11' in the Z23 profile. Systems built to the past answer will send and expect Z33.
    Differs
    Asked What should be sent back (and what MSH-21 profile identifier used) when there is an error in a query such as a Z44?
    Recommendation Confirm the change, record it in the change history, and say which query errors get a Z23 ACK and which a Z33 RSP (for example, an invalid QPD parameter). Z23 is also listed under the RSP profiles, though it is an ACK; say so.

    See the TA question

    qf-ta-q03-107
  • Note 2.1.3 Message Structure and Terminology: TA question (MSH Message Header, MSH-3 Sending Application): Gap. The SFT segment is optional in every profile, but the narrative never mentions it, so there is no guidance on where to identify the sending software.
    Gap
    Asked How can the software version of the IIS be encoded within the MSH header rather than in an SFT segment?
    Recommendation Add a sentence to a Message Header section that SFT identifies the sending software and its version, and that MSH-3 identifies the application, not its version.

    See the TA question

    qf-ta-q03-098
  • Note 2.2.3 Notes on Data Intermediaries: TA question (MSH Message Header, MSH-5 Receiving Application): Partly answers. MSH-5 and MSH-6 are RE in IGAMT, and the guide says intermediaries must be transparent. It does not say who defines their values, whether an IIS may reject a message when they are empty, or that the IZ Gateway does not change them (the IZ Gateway is out of scope).
    Partly answers
    Asked An IIS has a hard stop when MSH-5 or MSH-6 is blank or not the expected value, but neighboring IIS it will exchange with may not populate them. Is that the standard, and will the IZ Gateway populate them?

    See the TA question

    qf-ta-q14-009
  • Note 2.7.1 Organization Identifier Data Types: TA question (MSH Message Header, MSH-4 Sending Facility): Partly answers. The guide mentions OID-based identifiers only as a possible future use, and leaves assigning authorities to each IIS. There is no general guidance on when to use OIDs.
    Partly answers
    Asked How should OIDs be used properly in HL7 messages?

    See the TA question

    qf-ta-q03-041
  • Note 2.7.1 Organization Identifier Data Types: TA question (MSH Message Header, MSH-4 Sending Facility): Partly answers. Section 2.7.1 says HD.1 carries the organization identifier and the other HD components are optional. It does not say they must not carry a second identifier (past answer: move it to MSH-22 or the WSDL Facility ID).
    Partly answers
    Asked Is it appropriate to use MSH-4.2 and MSH-4.3 to carry additional facility identifiers?
    Recommendation Say that HD.2 and HD.3 qualify the identifier in HD.1 (its namespace) and are not for additional identifiers.

    See the TA question

    qf-ta-q03-043
  • Note 2.7.1 Organization Identifier Data Types: TA question (MSH Message Header, MSH-23 Receiving Responsible Organization): Partly answers. The IGAMT binding answers the identifier type question (HL70203). The narrative never mentions MSH-23, and 2.7.1 says the components after HD.1 are optional. The past advice not to require MSH-22 components 6 to 10 is not in the guide.
    Partly answers
    Asked An IIS plans to populate response MSH-23 with MSH-23.6 = a local assigning authority, MSH-23.7 = a local identifier type code (e.g., `VACMANPIN`), and MSH-23.10 = an ID echoed from query MSH-22.10. NIST reports the MSH-23.7 value is not a member of value set HL70203_IZ. Since base HL7 table 0203 has "suggested values", is a local value acceptable?

    See the TA question

    qf-ta-q19-008
  • Note 2.7.2 Submitting Organization (MSH-4 Sending Facility): TA question (MSH Message Header, MSH-4 Sending Facility): Differs. The guide answers how to represent several facilities: MSH-4 is the submitting organization operating the interface, and child facilities go in RXA-11.4 or ORC-17. The 2022 answer suggested copying RXA-11.4 into MSH-4, which the guide no longer allows.
    Differs
    Asked A site with several facilities sending through one interface (e.g., one clinic submitting for multiple locations) needs the right facility in MSH-4. How should multi-facility sites be represented, and can a message mover map the administered-at location (RXA-11.4) into MSH-4?

    See the TA question

    qf-ta-q03-099
  • Note 2.7.7 Use in Query Transactions (QBP/RSP): TA question (MSH Message Header, MSH-4 Sending Facility): Differs. The 2017 answer said an IIS need not require MSH-4 on queries if it does not use it. The guide now makes MSH-4 required (R) and says query messages SHALL include it. A deliberate change.
    Differs
    Asked Should practices send MSH-4 on QBP messages?

    See the TA question

    qf-ta-q02-078
  • Note 2.8.1 Message Header Date/Time (MSH-7): TA question (MSH Message Header, MSH-7 Date/Time of Message): Partly answers. 2.8.1 says consistent precision is required, but not what precision is allowed or that a receiver must accept fractional seconds.
    Partly answers
    Asked How should an IIS respond when timestamps include precision beyond seconds (e.g., milliseconds)?
    Recommendation State that receivers SHALL accept any precision the DTM data type allows, down to fractions of a second.

    See the TA question

    qf-ta-q02-109
  • Note 2.8.1 Message Header Date/Time (MSH-7): TA question (MSH Message Header, MSH-7 Date/Time of Message): Gap. The 2020 answer said not to accept messages dated far in the past. The guide says MSH-7 is not for clinical use but not whether an IIS may check it for plausibility, and 3.3.5 s2 discourages rejecting for minor conformance issues.
    Gap
    Asked What should the IIS do when it receives very old values in MSH-7?
    Recommendation Say whether an implausible MSH-7 (far past or future) is an Error, a Warning, or ignored.

    See the TA question

    qf-ta-q04-039
  • Note 3.3.2 Application-Level Scope of ACK Messages: TA question (MSH Message Header, MSH-10 Message Control ID): Gap. The guide requires one ACK for every VXU and has IIS deduplicate records, but says nothing about a message received twice (same MSH-10 and content): whether the IIS may reject it, and with what severity.
    Gap
    Asked An IIS rejects messages as duplicates when MSH-10 and message content match a previous message, to reduce processing load. Is this acceptable given testers may resubmit identical messages?
    Recommendation Say how an IIS acknowledges a duplicate message (accept and deduplicate, or reject with a Warning).

    See the TA question

    qf-ta-q06-001
  • Note 3.3.4.1 MSA-1 Guidance: TA question (MSH Message Header, MSH-11 Processing ID): Partly answers. MSH-11 is required and bound to HL70103, and the MSA-1 guidance mentions an unsupported processing ID. The guide does not say what an IIS does with 'T' or 'D' arriving at a production endpoint.
    Partly answers
    Asked A provider sends `T` (Training) in MSH-11 on VXUs to production, while its queries send `P`. The vaccinations are still recorded. Does sending `T` have any effect on the data?
    Recommendation State that production interfaces SHALL send 'P', and how an IIS treats other values (see ta-q33-020).

  • Note 3.5.2.1 Z23 - Return an Acknowledgement: TA question (MSH Message Header, MSH-21 Message Profile Identifier): Differs. The 2024 answer: run the query anyway with a Warning, or reject with a Z33 RSP (MSA-1 'AE', ERR on MSH-21). Under the guide, missing required header information gets a Z23 ACK, and 'AE' no longer means rejected. See ta-q03-107.

  • Note 6.1.4 Focus on Data Quality and Program Goals: TA question (MSH Message Header, MSH-3 Sending Application): By appeal. IGAMT gives no length for MSH-3, and the guide says not to enforce arbitrary rules or reject for minor conformance preferences. But it never says what the lengths that IGAMT does carry (MSH-8 up to 40, MSH-10 up to 199) mean, or that base-standard lengths are not normative.
    By appeal
    Asked Is there a maximum length for MSH-3 (Sending Application)? A local IG lists 20 characters but the CDC IG does not; where did the limit come from, and what maximum is recommended?
    Recommendation In 5.11 (compatibility with the base standard) or 4.1, say what a length in the profiles means, and that IIS should not reject a value only because it is longer than a base-standard length.

multi-verb-statement 18

Statements that combine different conformance verbs.

Show 18 findings
  • Warning 2.4.1 General Requirements: Statement combines MAY, SHALL. Each statement should carry one requirement so it can be tested and referenced on its own.
    IIS MAY offer additional SOAP endpoints for specialized or high-volume use but the primary endpoint SHALL support both operations.
    qf-multi-verb-statement-2683164e
  • Warning 2.4.2 Supported Operations: Statement combines SHALL, SHOULD. Each statement should carry one requirement so it can be tested and referenced on its own.
    Sending systems SHALL implement submitSingleMessage and SHOULD implement connectivityTest.
    qf-multi-verb-statement-d123a582
  • Warning 3.4.4.2 System-Initiated Query Scenarios: Statement combines MAY, SHALL. Each statement should carry one requirement so it can be tested and referenced on its own.
    Bulk or cohort queries MAY be implemented as a series of single-patient QBPs and SHALL be coordinated with the IIS in advance.

  • Warning 3.4.6 Selecting a Candidate Match: Statement combines SHOULD, SHALL NOT. Each statement should carry one requirement so it can be tested and referenced on its own.
    Querying systems SHOULD treat candidate selection as transitory and SHALL NOT permanently associate the IIS identifier with the local patient record without explicit user awareness and control.
    qf-multi-verb-statement-5276edf3
  • Warning 4.3.2 Fixed Code Sets (Stable Code Sets Anchored to Messaging Semantics): Statement combines SHALL, SHALL NOT. Each statement should carry one requirement so it can be tested and referenced on its own.
    Systems (including submitting systems and IIS) SHALL use only the values explicitly defined for fixed code sets and SHALL NOT introduce local extensions.

  • Warning 4.3.5 Handling Unrecognized Values: Statement combines SHOULD, SHALL. Each statement should carry one requirement so it can be tested and referenced on its own.
    IIS SHOULD be tolerant of unrecognized values in open and dynamic valuesets when the field usage is RE or O and SHALL avoid excessive, non-actionable notifications.
    qf-multi-verb-statement-d3716885
  • Warning 4.5.2.2 Official Registry Name: Statement combines SHOULD, SHOULD NOT. Each statement should carry one requirement so it can be tested and referenced on its own.
    If an 'L' name is provided, systems SHOULD place it in the first repetition of PID-5 to simplify processing. Systems SHOULD NOT send an empty first PID-5 repetition to reserve space for an 'L' name; the repetition should be omitted if no 'L' name is available, and alternate name types (e.g., alias or newborn placeholder) should be included as subsequent repetitions.
    qf-multi-verb-statement-5eb563b7

    IIS MAY standardize capitalization for internal comparison or display purposes but SHALL NOT alter the capitalization in transmitted data.

    For NK1-2, submitting systems SHALL populate the name type code (NK1-2.7) and SHOULD use the official registry ('L') name when known.

    IIS SHALL accept optional use codes and MAY ignore them if not operationally needed.
    qf-multi-verb-statement-396f5c82
  • Warning 4.5.7.3 Deceased Status: Statement combines MAY, SHOULD NOT. Each statement should carry one requirement so it can be tested and referenced on its own.
    Receipt of a new immunization event MAY be interpreted by IIS as evidence of current engagement, but SHOULD NOT override an explicitly reported deceased status.

manual 1

Found by a reviewer.

  • Issue 1.5 Conformance Verbs: In Figure 1, the SHALL NOT definition repeats the SHOULD NOT text ("A recommended prohibition… may do so if justified by local needs"). The text definition on this card is correct: SHALL NOT is a mandatory prohibition.qf-manual-figure-1-shall-not
